Proposition 6.3.7
.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional classical weight standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module. Let
v\in M
be a highest weight vector of weight
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
. Then, we have

\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v=\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}v.

Furthermore, for each
\zeta=(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})\in X^{\imath}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}v=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}(v+Y^{(2\nu)}v)\quad&\text{ if }\zeta=(\overline{\nu},\overline{\nu}),\\
\frac{1}{2}(v-Y^{(2\nu)}v)\quad&\text{ if }\zeta=(\overline{\nu},\overline{\nu-1}),\\
0\quad&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{split}

Proof.

The assertions follow from Propositions
4.4.5
and
6.3.1
.
∎

Proposition 6.3.8
.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional classical weight module equipped with a contragredient Hermitian inner product. Set

\displaystyle\begin{split}&L_{1}:=\{b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}\mid\widetilde{X}b=0\},\\
&L_{2}:=\{b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}\mid\widetilde{B}_{2}b=0\}.\end{split}

Then, the linear map

L_{2}\rightarrow L_{1};\ b\mapsto(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})b

is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}
-vector spaces, with inverse

L_{1}\rightarrow L_{2};\ b\mapsto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{2}=\overline{0}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}b.

Proof.

Write an orthogonal irreducible decomposition of
M
as

M=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{r}M_{k},\quad M_{k}\simeq V(\nu_{k}),\quad\nu_{k}\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}.

This induces an orthogonal decomposition

\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{r}\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{k},\quad\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{k}:=\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M_{k}}\simeq\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu_{k}).

Since both
\widetilde{X}
and
\widetilde{B}_{2}
preserve
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{k}
for all
k\in[1,r]
, it suffices to prove the assertion for the
M=V(\nu)
,
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
case.

When
M=V(\nu)
for some
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
, we have

L_{1}=\mathbb{C}b_{\nu}.

Furthermore, by Corollary
6.3.5
(
2
), we see that

L_{2}=\mathbb{C}(1+(-1)^{\nu}\widetilde{Y}^{2\nu})b_{\nu}.

Using Proposition
6.3.7
, we compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})(\sum_{\zeta_{2}=\overline{0}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}b_{\nu})&=\frac{1}{2}(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})(1+(-1)^{\nu}\widetilde{Y}^{2\nu})b_{\nu}\\
&=\frac{1}{2}(1+(-1)^{\nu}\widetilde{Y}^{2\nu})b_{\nu}+\frac{1}{2}(1-(-1)^{\nu}\widetilde{Y}^{2\nu})b_{\nu}\\
&=b_{\nu}.\end{split}

This, together with dimension consideration, proves the assertion.
∎

6.4.
Applications to the general
n\geq 3
case

In this subsection, we consider the general
n\geq 3
case; results obtained here are necessary for the
n=4
case. For a subset
J=\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{r}\}\subseteq I
, let
\mathbf{U}_{J}=\mathbf{U}_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{r}}
(resp.,
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{J}=\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{r}}
) denote the subalgebra generated by
E_{j},F_{j},K_{j}^{\pm 1}
,
j\in J
(resp.,
B_{j}
,
j\in J
). Then,
(J,\emptyset,\operatorname{id})
is a Satake subdiagram of
(I,\emptyset,\operatorname{id})
of type AI. Let
\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}_{J}=\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{r}}:=\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}(J)
denote the corresponding modified
\imath
quantum group.

For each
\eta=(\eta_{j})_{j\in J}\in(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})^{J}=X^{\imath}(J)
, let
\mathbf{1}_{J,\eta}=\mathbf{1}_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{r},\eta}
denote the corresponding idempotent in
\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}_{J}
. It acts on each
X^{\imath}
-weight module by

\mathbf{1}_{J,\eta}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{j}=\eta_{j}\text{ for all }j\in J\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}.

In particular, for each
\zeta=(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1})\in X^{\imath}
, we have

(45)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{1,\zeta_{1}}\cdots\mathbf{1}_{n-1,\zeta_{n-1}}=\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}

on each
X^{\imath}
-weight module.

Lemma 6.4.1
.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional classical weight standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module. Let
v\in M
be a highest weight vector of weight
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
. Then,
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v
is a standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module.

Proof.

For each
\zeta\in X^{\imath}
, by equation (
45
), we have

\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}v=\mathbf{1}_{1,\zeta_{1}}\cdots\mathbf{1}_{n-1,\zeta_{n-1}}v.

For a proof, by Propositions
3.3.8
and
4.4.4
, it suffices to show that
\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}v\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v
for all
\zeta\in X^{\imath}
.

Let
\zeta\in X^{\imath}
be such that
\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}v\neq 0
. We show that
\overline{\nu}_{2i-1}=\zeta_{2i-1}
for all
i\in I_{\mathfrak{k}}
and

\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}v=\frac{1}{2^{m^{\prime}}}(1-(-1)^{\delta_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}}Y_{2}^{(2\nu_{1})})(1-(-1)^{\delta_{\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4}}}Y_{4}^{(2\nu_{3})})\cdots(1-(-1)^{\delta_{\zeta_{2m^{\prime}-1},\zeta_{2m^{\prime}}}}Y_{2m^{\prime}}^{(2\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1})})v,

where

m^{\prime}:=\begin{cases}\frac{n}{2}-1\quad&\text{ if }n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}},\\
\frac{n-1}{2}\quad&\text{ if }n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}.\end{cases}

We proceed by induction on
n
. The case when
n=3
has been already proved in Proposition
6.3.7
.

First, consider the case when
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}
. Since
B_{n-1}v=[\nu_{n-1}]v
, we obtain

\mathbf{1}_{n-1,\zeta_{n-1}}v=\delta_{\overline{\nu_{n-1}},\zeta_{n-1}}v.

This, together with our hypothesis that
\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}v\neq 0
, implies that
\overline{\nu_{n-1}}=\zeta_{n-1}
. As a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{1,2,\ldots,n-2}
-module vector,
\mathbf{1}_{n-1,\zeta_{n-1}}v=v
is a highest weight vector of weight
(\nu_{1},\nu_{3},\ldots,\nu_{n-3})
. Therefore, our claim follows from the induction hypothesis.

Next, consider the case when
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}
. By the
n=3
case, we have

\mathbf{1}_{n-2,\zeta_{n-2}}\mathbf{1}_{n-1,\zeta_{n-1}}v=\delta_{\overline{\nu_{n-2}},\zeta_{n-2}}\frac{1}{2}(1-(-1)^{\delta_{\zeta_{n-2},\zeta_{n-1}}}Y_{n-1}^{(2\nu_{n-2})})v.

As before, this implies that
\overline{\nu_{n-2}}=\zeta_{n-2}
. By weight consideration, as a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{1,2,\ldots,n-3}
-module vector, both
v
and
Y_{n-1}^{(2\nu_{n-2})}v
are highest weight vectors of weight
(\nu_{1},\nu_{3},\ldots,\nu_{n-4})
. Then, our claim follows from the induction hypothesis. Thus, the proof completes.
∎

Proposition 6.4.2
.

Let
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}
. Then,
V(\nu)
is a standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Propositions
4.3.3
,
4.4.7
, and Lemma
6.4.1
.
∎

For each
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}
, set

V(\nu)_{\mathbf{A}}:=\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}_{\mathbf{A}}v_{\nu},

and call it the
\mathbf{A}
-form of
V(\nu)
.

For a based classical weight module
M
, consider the following condition:

(46)

\displaystyle G^{\imath}(b)\in M_{\nu}\oplus\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}\setminus\{\nu\}\\
|\xi_{i}|\geq|\nu_{i}|\text{ for all }i\in I_{\otimes}\end{subarray}}M_{\xi}\quad\text{ for all }\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}},b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M,\nu}.

When
M
satisfies this condition, for each
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}
and
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M,\nu}
, set
G^{\imath}_{0}(b)
to be the image of
G^{\imath}(b)
under the projection onto the weight space
M_{\nu}
. This defines a
\mathbb{C}
-linear map

G^{\imath}_{0}:\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}\rightarrow M

which preserves the
X_{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight spaces. Note that we have

\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(G^{\imath}_{0}(b))=b

for all
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}
.

Proposition 6.4.3
.

Let
(M,(\cdot,\cdot)_{M},M_{\mathbf{A}},\psi_{M},\mathcal{B}_{M})
be a finite-dimensional based
\mathbf{U}
-module such that
\psi^{\imath}_{M}:=\Upsilon\circ\psi_{M}
is defined. Then,
(M,(\cdot,\cdot)_{M},M_{\mathbf{A}},\psi^{\imath}_{M},\mathcal{B}_{M})
is a based classical weight standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module satisfying condition (
46
).

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a crystal basis of
M
. Let
i\in I
, and set

\mathcal{B}_{i}:=\{b\in\mathcal{B}\mid\widetilde{E}_{i}b=0\}.

Then, we have

\mathcal{B}=\{\widetilde{F}_{i}^{k}b\mid b\in\mathcal{B}_{i},\ k\in[0,\varphi_{i}(b)]\}.

Let
\preceq
be a total ordering on
\mathcal{B}_{i}
such that for each
b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
,
\operatorname{wt}(b_{1})<\operatorname{wt}(b_{2})
implies
b_{1}\prec b_{2}
. For each
b\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
, set

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}_{\succeq b}:=\{\widetilde{F}_{i}^{k}b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\mid b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{i},\ b^{\prime}\succeq b,\ k\in[0,\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime})]\},\\
&\mathcal{B}_{\succ b}:=\{\widetilde{F}_{i}^{k}b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\mid b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{i},\ b^{\prime}\succ b,\ k\in[0,\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime})]\},\\
&\mathcal{B}_{b}:=\mathcal{B}_{\succeq b}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{\succ b}=\{\widetilde{F}_{i}^{k}b\mid k\in[0,\varphi_{i}(b)]\}.\end{split}

Also, for each
b\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
and
\nu\in[-\varphi_{i}(b),\varphi_{i}(b)]_{p(\varphi_{i}(b))}
, set

b_{\nu}:=\begin{cases}b\quad&\text{ if }\nu=0,\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{F}_{i}^{|\nu|-1}b\pm\widetilde{F}_{i}^{|\nu|}b)\quad&\text{ if }\pm\nu>0.\end{cases}

By Proposition
2.6.7
, for each
b\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
, the
\mathbf{U}_{i}
-submodule
M_{\succeq b}
(resp.,
M_{\succ b}
) spanned by
\{G(b^{\prime})\mid b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{\succeq b}\}
(resp.,
\{G(b^{\prime})\mid b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{\succ b}\}
) is a based
\mathbf{U}_{i}
-submodule with a crystal basis
\mathcal{B}_{\succeq b}
(resp.,
\mathcal{B}_{\succ b}
). Furthermore, as a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{i}
-submodule, it is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{i}
-submodule by Lemma
3.2.3
and Proposition
3.4.9
(
1
).

Now, we prove by descending induction on
b\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
that
M_{\succeq b}
satisfies condition (
46
). Let
b\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
, and assume that our claim is true for all
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
such that
b^{\prime}\succ b
. Then, the quotient
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{i}
-module
M_{b}:=M_{\succeq b}/M_{\succ b}
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{i}
-module (by Proposition
3.4.9
(
2
)) isomorphic to the
(l+1)
-dimensional irreducible
\mathbf{U}_{i}
-module
V(l)
, where
l:=\varphi_{i}(b)
. Then, for each
\nu\in[-l,l]_{p(l)}
, we have

B_{i,\nu}[G^{\imath}(b)]=[G^{\imath}(b_{\nu})],

where
[v]
denotes the image of
v\in M_{\succeq b}
in
M_{b}
, and
B_{i,\nu}
is as in the proof of Proposition
5.2.1
. Let us write

B_{i,\nu}G^{\imath}(b)=G^{\imath}(b_{\nu})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{i}\\
b^{\prime}\succ b\end{subarray}}\sum_{\xi\in[-\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime}),\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime})]_{p(\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime}))}}c_{b^{\prime},\xi}G^{\imath}(b^{\prime}_{\xi}),

where
c_{b^{\prime},\xi}\in\mathbf{A}_{\operatorname{inv}}:=\{f\in\mathbf{A}\mid f(q^{-1})=f(q)\}
. We show that
c_{b^{\prime},\xi}=0
for all
\xi\notin\{\nu,\pm(|\nu|+2),\pm(|\nu|+4),\ldots\}
. Assume contrary and take
(b^{\prime},\xi)
such that
\deg(c_{b^{\prime},\xi})
is maximal. Let us write

G^{\imath}(b^{\prime}_{\xi})=\sum_{\omega\in X_{\mathfrak{k}}}m_{\omega},\quad m_{\omega}\in M_{\omega}.

Then, we have
m_{\omega}\in\mathcal{L}_{M}
,
\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(m_{\omega})=\delta_{\omega,\xi}b^{\prime}_{\xi}
. Consider

(B_{i,\nu}G^{\imath}(b),m_{\xi})_{M}.

Since the first factor is a sum of
B_{i}
-eigenvector of eigenvalues in
\{\nu,\pm(|\nu|+2),\pm(|\nu|+4),\ldots\}
, this value equals
0
. On the other hand, by the maximality of
\deg(c_{b^{\prime},\xi})
, this value belongs to
c_{b^{\prime},\xi}+q^{\deg(c_{b^{\prime},\xi})-1}\mathbf{K}_{\infty}
. This contradicts our assumption that
c_{b^{\prime},\xi}\neq 0
.

By above, we obtain that

G^{\imath}(b_{\nu})=B_{i,\nu}G^{\imath}(b)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{i}\\
b^{\prime}\succ b\end{subarray}}\sum_{\xi\in\{\nu,\pm(|\nu|+2),\ldots\}}c_{b^{\prime},\xi}G^{\imath}(b^{\prime}_{\xi}).

By the definition of
B_{i,\nu}
and our induction hypothesis, the right-hand side is a linear combination of
B_{i}
-eigenvectors of eigenvalues in
\{\nu,\pm(|\nu|+2),\ldots\}
. Therefore, we conclude that
M_{\succeq b}
satisfies condition (
46
), as desired. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.4.4
.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional based classical weight standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module satisfying condition (
46
). Let
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}
be such that
M[>\nu]=0
. Assume that
V(\nu)
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module. Then, there exists a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-submodule
N
of
M
isomorphic to
V(\nu)
.

Proof.

Let
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}[\nu]\cap\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M,\nu}
. Then, by condition (
46
) and our hypothesis that
M[>\nu]=0
, the vector
G^{\imath}(b)
is a highest weight vector of weight
\nu
. Set
N:=\mathbf{U}^{\imath}G^{\imath}(b)
. Then, there exists an isomorphism
\phi:V(\nu)\rightarrow N
of
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-modules which sends
v_{\nu}
to
G^{\imath}(b)
. It almost preserves the metrics. In particular, it induces an isomorphism
\phi:\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{N}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}
of
\mathbb{C}
-vector spaces.

For each
b^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)
, there exists
x_{b^{\prime}}\in\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}_{\mathbf{A}}
such that
x_{b^{\prime}}v_{\nu}=G^{\imath}(b^{\prime})
. Replacing
x_{b^{\prime}}
with
\frac{1}{2}(x_{b^{\prime}}+\psi^{\imath}(x_{b^{\prime}}))
, we may assume that
\psi^{\imath}(x_{b^{\prime}})=x_{b^{\prime}}
. Then, we have

\psi^{\imath}_{M}(\phi(G^{\imath}(b^{\prime})))=\psi^{\imath}_{M}(\phi(x_{b^{\prime}}v_{\nu}))=\psi^{\imath}_{M}(x_{b^{\prime}}G^{\imath}(b))=x_{b^{\prime}}G^{\imath}(b)=\phi(x_{b^{\prime}}v_{\nu})=\phi(G^{\imath}(b^{\prime})).

Furthermore, since
\phi
almost preserves the metrics, we obtain

\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(\phi(G^{\imath}(b^{\prime})))=\phi(\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(G^{\imath}(b^{\prime})))=\phi(b^{\prime}).

Then, by Lemma
3.4.6
, we conclude that

\phi(G^{\imath}(b^{\prime}))=G^{\imath}(\phi(b^{\prime})).

This far, we have shown that
N
is spanned by
G^{\imath}(\phi(\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)))
. Since
\phi(\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu))=\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{N}
, by Proposition
3.4.9
(
1
), we finally see that
N
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-submoule of
M
.
∎

7.
n=4
case

In this section, we consider the
n=4
case. In this case, we can identify
X=\mathbb{Z}^{3}
,
X^{+}=\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{\geq 0}
,
X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}=\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,
X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}=\{(\nu_{1},\nu_{3})\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}\mid\nu_{1}\geq|\nu_{3}|\}
, and
X^{\imath}=(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})^{3}
.

7.1.
Lowering and raising operators

Set

X_{\pm}:=X_{2,\pm}=B_{2,+\pm}\{l_{1};0\},\quad Y_{\pm}:=Y_{2,\pm}=B_{2,-\mp}\{l_{3};0\}.

Let
\nu=(\nu_{1},\nu_{3})\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
, and consider the irreducible
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module
V(\nu)
.

Lemma 7.1.1
.

For each
l,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&Y_{+}^{(m)}Y_{-}^{(l)}v_{\nu}=Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m)}v_{\nu},\\
&X_{-}Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m)}v_{\nu}=\frac{[\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}-l+1]\{\nu_{1}-l+1\}\{\nu_{3}+l\}}{\{\nu_{1}-l-m+1\}\{\nu_{3}+l-m\}}Y_{-}^{(l-1)}Y_{+}^{(m)}v_{\nu},\\
&X_{+}Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m)}v_{\nu}=\frac{[\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}-m+1]\{\nu_{1}-m+1\}\{\nu_{3}-m\}}{\{\nu_{1}-l-m+1\}\{\nu_{3}+l-m\}}Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m-1)}v_{\nu},\end{split}

where
Y_{\pm}^{(k)}:=\frac{1}{[k]!}Y_{\pm}^{k}
.

Proof.

By equations (
16
)–(
19
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&[Y_{+},Y_{-}]=0,\\
&[X_{\pm},Y_{\mp}\frac{\{l_{1};0\}}{\{l_{3};0\}}]=0,\\
&[X_{+},Y_{+}]=[l_{1}l_{3};0]+(q-q^{-1})^{2}Y_{-}X_{-}\frac{[l_{1}l_{3};0]}{\{l_{1};0\}\{l_{3};-1\}},\\
&[X_{-},Y_{-}]=[l_{1}l_{3}^{-1};0]+(q-q^{-1})^{2}Y_{+}X_{+}\frac{[l_{1}l_{3}^{-1};0]}{\{l_{1};0\}\{l_{3};1\}}.\end{split}

For each
m\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&X_{\pm}Y_{\mp}^{(m)}=Y_{\mp}^{(m)}X_{\pm}\frac{\{l_{1};1\}\{l_{3};0\}}{\{l_{1};-m+1\}\{l_{3};\pm m\}},\\
&X_{\pm}Y_{\pm}^{(m)}=Y_{\pm}^{(m-1)}[l_{1}l_{3}^{\pm 1};-m+1]+Y_{\pm}^{(m)}X_{\pm}+Y_{\pm}^{(m-1)}Y_{\mp}X_{\mp}P_{\pm}(m),\end{split}

for some rational function
P_{\pm}(m)\in\mathbb{Q}(l_{1},l_{3})
in variables
l_{1},l_{3}
. Now, it is straightforward to obtain the desired equations by induction on
l,m
.
∎

From this lemma (with a standard argument), we see that the set

\{Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m)}v_{\nu}\mid l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}],\ m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}]\}

forms a basis of
V(\nu)
. Note that
Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m)}v_{\nu}\in V(\nu)_{(\nu_{1}-l-m,\nu_{3}+l-m)}
. Then, for each
l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]
,
m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}]
, we can choose
c_{l,m,\nu}\in\mathbf{K}^{\times}
in a way such that
\operatorname{lt}(c_{l,m,\nu}Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m)}v_{\nu})=1
. For each
l,m\in\mathbb{Z}
, set

\widetilde{Y}_{-}^{l}\widetilde{Y}_{+}^{m}v_{\nu}:=\begin{cases}c_{l,m,\nu}Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m)}v_{\nu}\quad&\text{ if }l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]\and m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}],\\
0\quad&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then,
\{\widetilde{Y}_{-}^{l}\widetilde{Y}_{+}^{m}v_{\nu}\mid l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}],\ m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}]\}
forms an almost orthonormal basis of
V(\nu)
.

Using Lemma
7.1.1
, we see by induction on
l
and
m
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m)}v_{\nu},Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m)}v_{\nu})_{\nu}\\
&\quad={\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}\brack l}{\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}\brack m}\frac{\{\nu_{3}-m\}}{\{\nu_{3}+l-m\}}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\frac{\{\nu_{1}-i+1\}\{\nu_{3}+i\}}{\{\nu_{1}-i-m+1\}\{\nu_{1}-i-m\}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\{\nu_{3}-j+1\}}{\{\nu_{1}-j\}}.\end{split}

Lemma 7.1.2
.

For each
k\geq 0
, we have

Y^{(k)}=\sum_{l+m=k}Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m)}\frac{\{l_{3};l-m\}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\{l_{1};-j+1\}}{\prod_{j=0}^{k}\{l_{3};l-j\}}.

Proof.

By equation (
15
), we have

Y=(Y_{-}+Y_{+})\frac{\{l_{1};0\}}{\{l_{3};0\}}.

Then, the assertion follows by induction on
k
.
∎

Set

Y^{(l,m)}v_{\nu}:=\frac{\{\nu_{3}+l-m\}}{\{\nu_{1}-l-m\}}\prod_{j=0}^{l+m}\frac{\{\nu_{1}-j\}}{\{\nu_{3}+l-j\}}Y_{-}^{(l)}Y_{+}^{(m)}v_{\nu}.

By Lemma
7.1.2
, we see that

(47)

\displaystyle Y^{(k)}v_{\nu}=\sum_{l+m=k}Y^{(l,m)}v_{\nu}.

Also, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(Y^{(l,m)}v_{\nu},Y^{(l,m)}v_{\nu})_{\nu}\\
&\quad={\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}\brack l}{\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}\brack m}\frac{\{\nu_{1}\}\{\nu_{3}+l-m\}}{\{\nu_{3}\}\{\nu_{1}-l-m\}}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\frac{\{\nu_{1}-i+1\}}{\{\nu_{3}+l-i+1\}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\{\nu_{1}-j+1\}}{\{\nu_{3}-j\}}.\end{split}

This implies that for each
l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]
and
m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}]
, we have

(48)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\deg(Y^{(l,m)}v_{\nu})\\
&\quad=\frac{1}{2}((\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}-l)l+(\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}-m)m+\nu_{1}-|\nu_{3}|-|\nu_{1}-l-m|+|\nu_{3}+l-m|\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{l}(|\nu_{1}-i+1|-|\nu_{3}+l-i+1|)+\sum_{j=1}^{m}(|\nu_{1}-j+1|-|\nu_{3}-j|)).\end{split}

Definition 7.1.3
.

The lowering operator
\widetilde{Y}_{\pm}
and the raising operator
\widetilde{X}_{\pm}
are
\mathbf{K}
-linear endomorphism on
V(\nu)
defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{Y}_{+}(\widetilde{Y}_{-}^{l}\widetilde{Y}_{+}^{m}v_{\nu}):=\begin{cases}\widetilde{Y}_{-}^{l}\widetilde{Y}_{+}^{m+1}v_{\nu}\quad&\text{ if }l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]\and m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}],\\
0\quad&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{Y}_{-}(\widetilde{Y}_{-}^{l}\widetilde{Y}_{+}^{m}v_{\nu}):=\begin{cases}\widetilde{Y}_{-}^{l+1}\widetilde{Y}_{+}^{m}v_{\nu}\quad&\text{ if }l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]\and m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}],\\
0\quad&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{X}_{+}(\widetilde{Y}_{-}^{l}\widetilde{Y}_{+}^{m}v_{\nu}):=\begin{cases}\widetilde{Y}_{-}^{l}\widetilde{Y}_{+}^{m-1}v_{\nu}\quad&\text{ if }l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]\and m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}],\\
0\quad&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{X}_{-}(\widetilde{Y}_{-}^{l}\widetilde{Y}_{+}^{m}v_{\nu}):=\begin{cases}\widetilde{Y}_{-}^{l-1}\widetilde{Y}_{+}^{m}v_{\nu}\quad&\text{ if }l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]\and m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}],\\
0\quad&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{split}

Clearly, the operators
\widetilde{Y}_{\pm}
and
\widetilde{X}_{\pm}
preserve
\mathcal{L}(\nu)
. Hence, they induce linear operators
\widetilde{Y}_{\pm}
and
\widetilde{X}_{\pm}
on
\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)
.

Since each finite-dimensional classical weight module is completely reducible, we can extend the definitions of
\widetilde{Y}_{\pm},\widetilde{X}_{\pm}
on the irreducible modules to each finite-dimensional classical weight modules. The following can be proved in a similar way to the
n=3
case.

Proposition 7.1.4
.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional classical weight
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module. Let
\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}
. Then, we have
Y_{\pm}M_{\xi}=\widetilde{Y}_{\pm}M_{\xi}
.

Proposition 7.1.5
.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional classical weight
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module equipped with a contragredient Hermitian inner product. Then, for each
u,v\in\mathcal{L}_{M}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(\widetilde{Y}_{\pm}u,v)-(u,\widetilde{X}_{\pm}v)\in q^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{\infty}.\end{split}

7.2.
Based module structures of irreducible modules

For each
l,m\in\mathbb{Z}
, set

\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}:=\widetilde{Y}_{-}^{l}\widetilde{Y}_{+}^{m}.

Let
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
, and consider the irreducible module
V(\nu)
. Note that for each
l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]
and
m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}]
, we have

\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(\frac{1}{\operatorname{lt}(Y^{(l,m)}v_{\nu})}Y^{(l,m)}v_{\nu})=\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}b_{\nu}.

Lemma 7.2.1
.

Let
l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]
,
m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}]
be such that
l+m\leq\nu_{1}
. Then, we have

\deg(Y^{(l,m)}v_{\nu})=\deg(Y^{(\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}-l,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}-m)}v_{\nu}).

Proof.

By equation (
48
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\deg(Y^{(\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}-l,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}-m)}v_{\nu})\\
&=\frac{1}{2}((\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}-l)l+(\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}-m)m+\nu_{1}-|\nu_{3}|-|\nu_{1}-l-m|+|\nu_{3}+l-m|\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}-l}(|\nu_{1}-i+1|-|\nu_{1}-l-i+1|)+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}-m}(|\nu_{1}-j+1|-|\nu_{3}-j|)).\end{split}

Here, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{i=1}^{\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}-l}(|\nu_{1}-i+1|-|\nu_{1}-l-i+1|)\\
&=\begin{cases}\sum_{s=1}^{\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}-l}|\nu_{1}-s+1|-\sum_{s=1}^{\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}-l}|\nu_{1}-m-t+1|\quad&\text{ if }\nu_{1}-l\geq\nu_{3}+l,\\
(\sum_{s=1}^{l}|\nu_{1}-s+1|-\sum_{t=1}^{-\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}+2l}|\nu_{3}+l-t+1|)-\\
(\sum_{s=1}^{l}|\nu_{3}+l-s+1|-\sum_{t=1}^{-\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}+2l}|\nu_{3}+l-t+1|)\quad&\text{ if }\nu_{1}-l\leq\nu_{3}+l\end{cases}\\
&=\sum_{s=1}^{l}|\nu_{1}-s+1|-\sum_{s=1}^{l}|\nu_{3}+l-s+1|\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{l}(|\nu_{1}-i+1|-|\nu_{3}+l-i+1|).\end{split}

Similarly, we obtain

\sum_{j=1}^{\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}-m}(|\nu_{1}-j+1|-|\nu_{3}-j|)=\sum_{j=1}^{m}(|\nu_{1}-j+1|-|\nu_{3}-j|).

Thus, the assertion follows.
∎

Set
d_{\nu}^{l,m}:=\deg(Y^{(l,m)}v_{\nu})
. By direct calculation, we obtain the following.

Lemma 7.2.2
.

Let
l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]
,
m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}]
be such that
l+m\leq\nu_{1}
.

(1)

If
\nu_{3}+l-m\geq 0
, then we have

d_{\nu}^{l,m}-d_{\nu}^{l+1,m-1}=\begin{cases}2(\nu_{3}+l-m)+1>0\quad&\text{ if }\nu_{3}-m\leq 0,\\
(\nu_{3}+l-m)+l+1>0\quad&\text{ if }\nu_{3}-m\geq 0.\end{cases}

(2)

If
\nu_{3}+l-m\leq 0
, then we have

d_{\nu}^{l,m}-d_{\nu}^{l-1,m+1}=\begin{cases}-2(\nu_{3}+l-m)+1>0\quad&\text{ if }\nu_{3}+l\geq 0,\\
-(\nu_{3}+l-m)+m+1>0\quad&\text{ if }\nu_{3}+l\leq 0.\end{cases}

(3)

If
\nu_{3}+l-m=-1
, then we have

d_{\nu}^{l,m}=d_{\nu}^{l+1,m-1}.

Proposition 7.2.3
.

Let
k\in[0,2\nu_{1}]
.

(1)

Suppose that
\nu_{3}\geq 0
and set

(l_{0},m_{0}):=\begin{cases}(0,k)\quad&\text{ if }0\leq k\leq\nu_{3},\\
(\frac{k-\nu_{3}}{2},\frac{k+\nu_{3}}{2})\quad&\text{ if }\nu_{3}\leq k\leq 2\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}\and k-\nu_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}},\\
(\frac{k-\nu_{3}+1}{2},\frac{k+\nu_{3}-1}{2})\quad&\text{ if }\nu_{3}\leq k\leq 2\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}\and k-\nu_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}},\\
(\nu_{1}-\nu_{3},k-\nu_{1}+\nu_{3})\quad&\text{ if }2\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}\leq k\leq 2\nu_{1}.\end{cases}

Then, we have

\widetilde{Y}^{k}b_{\nu}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{Y}^{l_{0},m_{0}}+\widetilde{Y}^{l_{0}-1,m_{0}+1})b_{\nu}\quad&\text{ if }\nu_{3}\leq k\leq 2\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}\and k-\nu_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}},\\
\widetilde{Y}^{l_{0},m_{0}}b_{\nu}\quad&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(2)

Suppose that
\nu_{3}\leq 0
and set

(l_{0},m_{0}):=\begin{cases}(k,0)\quad&\text{ if }0\leq k\leq-\nu_{3},\\
(\frac{k-\nu_{3}}{2},\frac{k+\nu_{3}}{2})\quad&\text{ if }-\nu_{3}\leq k\leq 2\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}\and k+\nu_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}},\\
(\frac{k-\nu_{3}+1}{2},\frac{k+\nu_{3}-1}{2})\quad&\text{ if }-\nu_{3}\leq k\leq 2\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}\and k+\nu_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}},\\
(k-\nu_{1}-\nu_{3},\nu_{1}+\nu_{3})\quad&\text{ if }2\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}\leq k\leq 2\nu_{1}.\end{cases}

Then, we have

\widetilde{Y}^{k}b_{\nu}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{Y}^{l_{0},m_{0}}+\widetilde{Y}^{l_{0}-1,m_{0}+1})b_{\nu}\quad&\text{ if }-\nu_{3}\leq k\leq 2\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}\and k+\nu_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}},\\
\widetilde{Y}^{l_{0},m_{0}}b_{\nu}\quad&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof.

The assertion follows from the definition of
\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}
and Lemmas
7.2.1
and
7.2.2
.
∎

Lemma 7.2.4
.

Let
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
,
M
a based classical weight standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module satisfying condition (
46
). Suppose that
M[>\nu]=0
. Then,
M
contains a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-submodule isomorphic to
V(\nu)
which is also a based submodule of
M
.

Proof.

We prove the statement under the assumption that
\nu_{3}\geq 0
. The
\nu_{3}\leq 0
case can be proved similarly.

Let
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M,\nu}
and set
v:=G^{\imath}(b)
. By our hypothesis,
v
is a highest weight vector of weight
\nu
. Hence, in order to prove the assertion, it suffices to show that
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}b)\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v
for all
l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]
and
m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}]
. Below, we concentrate on the case when
l+m\leq\nu_{1}
. The case when
l+m\geq\nu_{1}
can be proved similarly by exchanging the roles of
v
and
Y^{(2\nu_{1})}v(=Y^{(\nu_{1}-\nu_{3},\nu_{1}+\nu_{3})}v)
.

Set

B:=\{\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}v\mid l\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}],m\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}]\}.

Then, it forms a basis of
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v
consisting of vectors in
\mathcal{L}_{M}
. Let
\widetilde{B}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{M}
be a basis of
M
which extends
B
.

We show that
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}b)\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v
inductively. Assume that we have
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}b)\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v
for the following cases:

•

|\nu_{1}-(l^{\prime}+m^{\prime})|>\nu_{1}-(l+m)
, or

•

l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}=l+m
and
|\nu_{3}+l^{\prime}-m^{\prime}|<|\nu_{3}+l-m|
.

For such
l^{\prime},m^{\prime}
, we can write

G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}b)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l^{\prime\prime}\in[0,\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}]\\
m^{\prime\prime}\in[0,\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}]\end{subarray}}p^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}v

for some
p^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{K}_{\infty}
. By condition (
46
), we see that
p^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}=0
if

•

|\nu_{1}-(l^{\prime\prime}+m^{\prime\prime})|<|\nu_{1}-(l^{\prime}+m^{\prime})|
, or

•

|\nu_{3}+l^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime\prime}|<|\nu_{3}+l^{\prime}-m^{\prime}|
.

Assume further that for each
l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}
such that
l^{\prime\prime}+m^{\prime\prime}=l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}
and
|\nu_{3}+l^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime\prime}|\geq|\nu_{3}+l^{\prime}-m^{\prime}|
, we have

\deg(p^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}})\leq d_{\nu}^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}-d_{\nu}^{l^{\prime}_{0},m^{\prime}_{0}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\tilde{k}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}}(|\nu_{3}+l^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime\prime}|-j),

where
(l^{\prime}_{0},m^{\prime}_{0})
is as in Lemma
7.2.3
with
l
being replaced with
l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}
, and

\tilde{k}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}:=\begin{cases}|l^{\prime}-l^{\prime}_{0}|\quad&\text{ if }l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}\leq\nu_{3}\text{ or }l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}\geq 2\nu_{1}-\nu_{3},\\
|\nu_{3}+l^{\prime}-m^{\prime}|\quad&\text{ if }\nu_{3}\leq l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}\leq 2\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}\and|\nu_{3}+m^{\prime}-n^{\prime}|\leq l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}-\nu_{3},\\
l^{\prime}\quad&\text{ if }\nu_{3}\leq l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}\leq 2\nu_{1}-\nu_{3}\and|\nu_{3}+l^{\prime}-m^{\prime}|\geq l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}-\nu_{3}.\end{cases}

Under the induction hypothesis above, we show that
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}b)\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v
. Before doing so, let us prepare some notation. Set
k:=l+m
. Then, we can write as

\widetilde{Y}^{k}v=\sum_{l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}=k}r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}v

for some
r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{K}_{\infty}
such that

\operatorname{lt}(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}})=q^{d_{\nu}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}-d_{\nu}^{l_{0},m_{0}}}.

Also, for each
j\geq 0
, we have

B_{3}^{(j)}\widetilde{Y}^{k}v=\sum_{l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}=k}r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},j}\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}v,

where
r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},j}:=r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\frac{[\nu_{3}+l^{\prime}-m^{\prime}]^{j}}{[j]!}
. Hence, we have

\operatorname{lt}(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},j})=q^{d_{\nu}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}-d_{\nu}^{l_{0},m_{0}}+\sum_{i=1}^{j}(|\nu_{3}+l^{\prime}-m^{\prime}|-i)}.

Note that we have

(49)

\displaystyle\deg(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},0})<\deg(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},1})<\cdots<\deg(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},|\nu_{3}+l^{\prime}-m^{\prime}|-1}).

Let us prove that
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}b)\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v
. First, suppose that
k\leq\nu_{3}
. In this case, we have

(l_{0},m_{0})=(0,k),\and\tilde{k}_{l,m}=l.

Using Lemma
7.2.2
, we see that for each
l^{\prime},m^{\prime}
such that
l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}=k
and for
j\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\deg(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},j})&=-\frac{1}{2}l^{\prime}(2(\nu_{3}-m^{\prime})+l^{\prime}-1)+\frac{1}{2}j(2(\nu_{3}-m^{\prime})+2l^{\prime}-j-1)\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}(l^{\prime}-j)(2(\nu_{3}-m^{\prime})+l^{\prime}-j-1).\end{split}

This implies that

(50)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\deg(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},l^{\prime}})=0,\\
&\deg(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},j})<0\quad\text{ if }j<l^{\prime}.\end{split}

By Theorem
6.3.4
, we have

G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{0,k}b)=G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v.

By the proof of Proposition
6.3.3
, we see that

G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)\in\widetilde{Y}^{k}v+\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}j<k\text{ or }j>2\nu_{1}-k\\
\overline{j}=\overline{k}\end{subarray}}q^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{\infty}\widetilde{Y}^{j}v.

This shows that
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)
is a sum of
B_{3}
-eigenvectors of eigenvalues
[a]
,
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p(\nu_{3}-k)}
. Since
M
is a standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module, we see that

\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)=0

for all
\zeta\in X^{\imath}
such that
\zeta_{3}\neq\overline{\nu_{3}-k}
. Therefore, we obtain

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{3}=\overline{\nu_{3}-k}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)=\sum_{\zeta\in X^{\imath}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)=G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b).

Hence, we have

B_{3,p(\nu_{3}-k)}^{(l)}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)=\sum_{\zeta_{3}=\overline{\nu_{3}-k}}B_{3,\zeta}^{(l)}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)\in\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}_{\mathbf{A}}v.

By the definition of
\imath
divided powers, we can write as

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{3,p(\nu_{3}-k)}^{(l)}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)&=\sum_{j=0}^{l}c_{j}B_{3}^{(j)}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)\\
&\in\sum_{j=0}^{l}c_{j}B_{3}^{(j)}\widetilde{Y}^{k}v+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}<k\text{ or }\\
l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}>2\nu_{1}-k\end{subarray}}\mathbf{K}\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}v\end{split}

for some
c_{j}\in\delta_{j,l}+q^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{\infty}
. For each
j\in[0,l]
, set

u^{\prime}_{j}:=c_{j}B_{3}^{(j)}\widetilde{Y}^{k}v=\sum_{l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}=k}c_{j}r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},j}\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}v,

and

l^{\prime}:=\min\{l^{\prime\prime}\neq l\mid\deg(c_{j}r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},j})\geq 0\}.

By (
50
), we must have
l^{\prime}<j
. Set
m^{\prime}:=k-l^{\prime}
. Then, there exists
a_{l^{\prime},j}\in\mathbf{A}_{\operatorname{inv}}
such that
u^{\prime}_{j}-a_{l^{\prime},j}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}b)
, expanded by the basis
B
, contains
\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}v
with coefficient in
q^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{\infty}
. Note that we have

\deg(a_{l^{\prime},j})=\deg(c_{j}r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},j}).

Then, for each
l^{\prime\prime}>l^{\prime}
, setting
m^{\prime\prime}:=k-l^{\prime\prime}
, we compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\deg(c_{j}r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},j})-\deg(a_{l^{\prime},j}p^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}})\\
&\quad=\deg(c_{j})+\deg(r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},j})-(\deg(c_{j})+\deg(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},j})+\deg(p^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}))\\
&\quad=\deg(r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},j})-\deg(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},j})-\deg(p^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}})\\
&\quad\geq\deg(r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},j})-\deg(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},j})-\deg(r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},l^{\prime}})\\
&\quad=d_{\nu}^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}-d_{\nu}^{l_{0},m_{0}}+j(\nu_{3}+l^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime\prime}-1)-\frac{1}{2}j(j+1)\\
&\quad\quad-(d_{\nu}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}-d_{\nu}^{l_{0},m_{0}}+j(\nu_{3}+l^{\prime}-m^{\prime}-1)-\frac{1}{2}j(j+1))\\
&\quad\quad-(d_{\nu}^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}-d_{\nu}^{l_{0},m_{0}}+l^{\prime}(\nu_{3}+l^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime\prime}-1)-\frac{1}{2}l^{\prime}(l^{\prime}+1))\\
&\quad=(j-l^{\prime})(\nu_{3}+l^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime\prime}-1)-\deg(r^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime},l^{\prime}})-(j-l^{\prime})(\nu_{3}+l^{\prime}-m^{\prime}-1)\\
&\quad=(j-l^{\prime})((l^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime\prime})-(l^{\prime}-m^{\prime}))>0.\end{split}

Therefore, both
u^{\prime}
and
u^{\prime}-a_{l^{\prime},j}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}b)
contain
\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}v
,
l^{\prime\prime}>l^{\prime}
,
l^{\prime\prime}+m^{\prime\prime}=k
with coefficient in
q^{\deg(c_{j}r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},j})}\mathbf{K}_{\infty}
.

Replacing
u^{\prime}
with
u^{\prime}-a_{l^{\prime},j}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}b)
, and repeating the procedure above, we finally obtain that

u_{j}:=c_{j}B_{3}^{(j)}\widetilde{Y}^{k}v-\sum_{l^{\prime}=0}^{j-1}a_{l^{\prime},j}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}b)

contains
\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}v
,
l^{\prime\prime}<j
,
l^{\prime\prime}+m^{\prime\prime}=k
with coefficient in
q^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{\infty}
, and
\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}v
,
l^{\prime\prime}\geq j
,
l^{\prime\prime}+m^{\prime\prime}=k
with coefficient in
q^{\deg(c_{j}r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},j})}\mathbf{K}_{\infty}
, where
a_{l^{\prime},j}\in\mathbf{A}_{\operatorname{inv}}
.

By above, we see that

u:=B_{3,p(\nu_{3}+l-m)}^{(l)}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)-\sum_{j=0}^{l}\sum_{l^{\prime}=0}^{j-1}a_{l^{\prime},j}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},k-l^{\prime}}b)\in\sum_{j=0}^{l}u_{j}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}<k\text{ or }\\
l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}>2\nu_{1}-k\end{subarray}}\mathbf{K}\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}v

contains
\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}v
,
l^{\prime\prime}<l
,
l^{\prime\prime}+m^{\prime\prime}=k
with coefficient in
q^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{\infty}
, and
\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}}v
,
l^{\prime\prime}\geq l
,
l^{\prime\prime}+m^{\prime\prime}=k
with the degree of coefficient being

\max_{0\leq j\leq l}\{\deg(c_{j}r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},j})\}.

Since
m^{\prime\prime}\leq k\leq\nu_{3}
, we have
\nu_{3}+l^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime\prime}\geq l^{\prime\prime}\geq l
. Then, by equation (
49
), we see that

\max_{0\leq j\leq l}\{\deg(c_{j}r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},j})\}=\deg(r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},l})\leq 0;

the equality holds if and only if
l^{\prime\prime}=l
by equation (
50
).

By the construction above,
u-G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}b)
is contained in
M_{\mathbf{A}}
, and is invariant under
\psi^{\imath}_{M}
. Furthermore, the vector
u
, expanded by
\widetilde{B}
, contains
w\in\widetilde{B}
with coefficient in
q^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{\infty}
unless
w=\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}v
with
l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}<k
or
l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}>2\nu_{1}-k
. From these observations and condition (
46
), we can find
a^{\prime}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{A}_{\operatorname{inv}}
such that

u-G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}b)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}<k\text{ or }\\
l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}>2\nu_{1}-k\end{subarray}}a^{\prime}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}b)\in q^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{M}\cap M_{\mathbf{A}}.

Then, by Lemma
3.4.6
, we see that

u-G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}b)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}<k\text{ or }\\
l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}>2\nu_{1}-k\end{subarray}}a^{\prime}_{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}b)=0.

This shows that

G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}b)\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v,

as desired. By the construction, the degree of
\widetilde{Y}^{l^{\prime},m^{\prime}}v
,
l^{\prime}>l
,
l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}=k
in
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}b)
is at most
\deg(r^{l^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime},l})
. Hence, we can proceed our induction.

Next, suppose that
\nu_{3}\leq k\leq\nu_{1}
and
k-\nu_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}
(the case when
k-\nu_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}
is similar). By the same argument as above, we obtain

G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l_{0},m_{0}}b)=G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b)\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v.

Computing
B_{3,\mathrm{ev}}^{(\tilde{k}_{m,n})}G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{k}b_{\nu})
, one can construct
G^{\imath}((\widetilde{Y}^{l_{+},m_{+}}\pm\widetilde{Y}^{l_{-},m_{-}})b)
if
|\nu_{3}+l-m|\leq k-\nu_{3}
, and
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}^{l,m}b)
if
\nu_{3}+l-m>k-\nu_{3}
in the same way as above, where
(l_{\pm},m_{\pm})
is such that
l_{\pm}+m_{\pm}=k
and

\nu_{3}+l_{+}-m_{+}=-(\nu_{3}+l_{-}-m_{-})=|\nu_{3}+l-m|

This completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 7.2.5
.

Let
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
. Then,
V(\nu)
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module.

Proof.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional based classical weight standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module satisfying condition (
46
) such that
M[\nu]\neq 0
. Such
M
surely exists by Propositions
4.3.3
and
4.4.7
. Let us write an irreducible decomposition of
M
as

M=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{r}M_{k},\quad M_{k}\simeq V(\nu_{k}),\quad\nu_{k}\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
k\leq l
implies
\nu_{k}\leq\nu_{l}
. By Lemma
7.2.4
, we see that
M_{r}
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-submodule of
M
, and hence,
V(\nu_{r})
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module. Then,
M/M_{r}
is a finite-dimensional based classical weight standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module satisfying condition (
46
) by Propositions
3.3.7
(
2
) and
3.4.9
(
2
). Replacing
M
with
M/M_{r}
, we see that
V(\nu_{r-1})
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module. Proceeding in this way, we conclude that
V(\nu_{k})
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module for all
k\in[1,r]
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.2.6
.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional classical weight
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module equipped with a contragredient Hermitian inner product. Set

\displaystyle\begin{split}&L_{1}:=\{b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}\mid\widetilde{X}_{+}b=\widetilde{X}_{-}b=0\},\\
&L_{2}:=\mathbb{C}\{b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}\mid\text{$b$ is $B_{1}$-homogeneous,}\ \widetilde{B}_{2}b=0,\and\deg_{3}((\widetilde{B}_{2}\widetilde{B}_{1})^{\deg_{3}(b)}b)=0\}.\end{split}

Then, the linear map

L_{2}\rightarrow L_{1};\ b\mapsto(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})b

is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}
-vector spaces with inverse

L_{1}\rightarrow L_{2};\ b\mapsto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{2}=\overline{0}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}b.

Proof.

As the proof of Proposition
6.3.8
, it suffices to consider the case when
M=V(\nu)
for some
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
. Clearly, we have

L_{1}=\mathbb{C}b_{\nu}.

Let
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)
be
\widetilde{B}_{1}
-homogeneous such that
\widetilde{B}_{2}b=0
and
\deg_{3}((\widetilde{B}_{2}\widetilde{B}_{1})^{\deg_{3}(b)}b)=0
. Set
l_{1}:=\deg_{1}(b)
and
l_{3}:=\deg_{3}(b)
. By Proposition
6.3.8
, there exists
b^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)
such that
b^{\prime}
is a sum of
X_{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight vectors of weights
\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}
with
\xi_{1}=l_{1}
, and such that
\widetilde{X}b^{\prime}=0
and
b=(1+(-1)^{l_{1}}\widetilde{Y}^{2l_{1}})b^{\prime}
. Then, from Corollary
6.3.5
, we see that

(\widetilde{B}_{2}\widetilde{B}_{1})^{l_{3}}b\in\mathbb{C}(\widetilde{Y}^{l_{3}}+(-1)^{l_{1}+l_{3}}\widetilde{Y}^{2l_{1}-l_{3}})b^{\prime}.

On the other hand, by Proposition
7.2.3
, we see that for each
b^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)
, we have
\deg_{3}(b^{\prime\prime})=0
only if
b^{\prime\prime}\in\bigoplus_{n=0}^{2\nu_{1}}\mathbb{C}\widetilde{Y}^{n}b_{\nu}
. Therefore, we obtain
l=\nu_{1}
,
b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}b_{\nu}
, and hence,

L_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{C}(1+(-1)^{\nu_{1}}\widetilde{Y}^{2\nu_{1}})b_{\nu}.

Conversely, by Proposition
7.2.3
again, we have

\deg_{3}((\widetilde{B}_{2}\widetilde{B}_{1})^{|\nu_{3}|}(1+(-1)^{\nu_{1}}\widetilde{Y}^{2\nu_{1}})b_{\nu})=\deg_{3}((\widetilde{Y}^{|\nu_{3}|}+(-1)^{\nu_{1}+|\nu_{3}|}\widetilde{Y}^{2\nu_{1}-|\nu_{3}|})b_{\nu})=0.

Thus, we see that

L_{2}=\mathbb{C}(1+(-1)^{\nu_{1}}\widetilde{Y}^{2\nu_{1}})b_{\nu}.

Now, by the same calculation as in the proof of Proposition
6.3.8
, the assertion follows.
∎

8.
General case

In this section, we consider the general
n\geq 3
case, and set

m:=\begin{cases}\frac{n}{2}\quad&\text{ if }n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}},\\
\frac{n-1}{2}\quad&\text{ if }n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}.\end{cases}

Let
j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, and set

J=\begin{cases}\{2i-1,2i,2i+1\}\quad&\text{ if }j=(2i,\pm),\\
\{2m-1,2m\}\quad&\text{ if }j=2m.\end{cases}

Then,
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{J}
is canonically isomorphic to the
\imath
quantum group of type AI with
n=2
or
3
. Hence, we can define linear operators
\widetilde{X}_{j},\widetilde{Y}_{j}
to correspond
\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y}
if
j=2m
or
\widetilde{X}_{\pm},\widetilde{Y}_{\pm}
if
j=(2i,\pm)
.

8.1.
Based module structure of irreducible modules

In this subsection, we prove that
V(\nu)
,
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module.

Lemma 8.1.1
.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional based classical weight standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module satisfying condition (
46
). Let
N\subseteq M
be a
\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}
-submodule,
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}
, and
j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}
. Suppose that for each
\xi\geq\nu
and
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{N,\xi}
we have
G^{\imath}(b)\in N
. Then, for each
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{N,\nu}
, we have
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}_{j}b)\in N
.

Proof.

Define
J\subseteq I
as above. Let us view
M
and
N
as
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{J}
-modules. Then, we have an orthogonal decomposition

\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{N}=\bigoplus_{\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}(J)}\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{N}[\xi].

Let
\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{r}\}
be an orthonormal basis of
\bigoplus_{\xi\geq\nu}(\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{N}[\xi]\cap\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{N,\xi})
consisting of
X_{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight vectors. Let
\xi_{k}\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}(J)
be such that
b_{k}\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{N,\xi_{k}}
. We may assume that
k\leq l
implies
\xi_{k}\leq\xi_{l}
. Note that we have

\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{N,\nu}\subseteq\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\widetilde{Y}_{j_{1}}\cdots\widetilde{Y}_{j_{s}}b_{k}\mid s\geq 0,\ k\in[1,r],\ j_{1},\ldots,j_{s}\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}(J)\}.

Hence, to prove the assertion, it suffices to show that
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}_{j_{1}}\cdots\widetilde{Y}_{j_{s}}b_{k})\in N
for all
s,k,j_{1},\ldots,j_{s}
.

By weight consideration,
G^{\imath}(b_{r})\in N
is a highest weight vector of weight
\xi_{r}
. Then, by Theorems
6.3.4
and
7.2.5
,
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{J}G^{\imath}(b_{r})
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{J}
-submodule of
M
, and
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}_{j_{1}}\cdots\widetilde{Y}_{j_{s}}b_{r})\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{J}G^{\imath}(b_{r})\subseteq N
. Replacing
M
with
M/\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{J}G^{\imath}(b_{r})
and repeating the same argument as above, we see that
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}_{j_{1}}\cdots\widetilde{Y}_{j_{s}}b_{r-1})\in N
for all
j_{1},\ldots,j_{s}\in J
. Proceeding in this way, we conclude that
G^{\imath}(\widetilde{Y}_{j_{1}}\cdots\widetilde{Y}_{j_{s}}b_{k})\in N
for all
s\geq 0
,
k\in[1,r]
,
j_{1},\ldots,j_{s}\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}(J)
, as desired.
∎

Theorem 8.1.2
.

Let
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
. Then,
V(\nu)
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module. Moreover, we have the following:

(1)

\mathcal{L}(\nu)=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbf{K}_{\infty}}\{\widetilde{Y}_{j_{1}}\cdots\widetilde{Y}_{j_{r}}v_{\nu}\mid r\geq 0,\ j_{1},\ldots,j_{r}\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}\}
.

(2)

\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\widetilde{Y}_{j_{1}}\cdots\widetilde{Y}_{j_{r}}b_{\nu}\mid r\geq 0,\ j_{1},\ldots,j_{r}\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}\}
.

(3)

For each
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)
, if
\widetilde{X}_{j}b=0
for all
j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, then we have
b\in\mathbb{C}b_{\nu}
.

Proof.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional based classical weight standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module satisfying condition (
46
) such that
M[\nu]\neq 0
. We proceed by descending induction on
\{\nu^{\prime}\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}\mid M[\nu^{\prime}]\neq 0\}
that
V(\nu^{\prime})
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module. Assuming that our claim is true for all
\nu^{\prime}>\nu
and replacing
M
with
M/M[>\nu]
, we may assume that
M[>\nu]=0
. Let
b_{0}\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M,\nu}
. Then,
G^{\imath}(b_{0})
is a highest weight vector of weight
\nu
. Hence, we can identify
V(\nu)
with an
X^{\imath}
-weight submodule
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}G^{\imath}(b_{0})
of
M
. Under this identification, we have
b_{0}=b_{\nu}
.

Let
\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}
be such that
\xi<\nu
. Assume that for all
\xi^{\prime}>\xi
and
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\xi^{\prime}}
, we have
G^{\imath}(b)\in V(\nu)
. Then,
V(\nu)_{\xi^{\prime}}
is spanned by
G^{\imath}_{0}(\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\xi^{\prime}})
. Actually, for a basis
\mathcal{B}
of
\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\xi^{\prime}}
,
G^{\imath}_{0}(\mathcal{B})
forms a basis of
V(\nu)_{\xi^{\prime}}
.

Let
v\in\mathcal{L}(\nu)_{\xi}
, and set
b:=\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(v)\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\xi}
. Since we have

V(\nu)_{\xi}=\sum_{j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}}Y_{j}V(\nu)_{\xi+\gamma_{j}}=\sum_{j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}}\widetilde{Y}_{j}V(\nu)_{\xi+\gamma_{j}}

by Theorem
4.2.5
(
7
) and Propositions
6.1.4
and
7.1.4
, we can write as

v=\sum_{j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}}v_{j},\quad v_{j}\in\widetilde{Y}_{j}V(\nu)_{\xi+\gamma_{j}}.

We show that we can take
v_{j}\in\mathcal{L}(\nu)
for all
j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}
.

For each
j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, choose an orhtonormal basis
\mathcal{B}_{j}
of
\widetilde{Y}_{j}\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\xi+\gamma_{j}}
. By Lemma
8.1.1
,
G^{\imath}(\mathcal{B}_{j})\subseteq V(\nu)
. Consequently,
G^{\imath}_{0}(\mathcal{B}_{j})
forms an almost orthonormal basis of
\widetilde{Y}_{j}V(\nu)_{\xi+\gamma_{j}}
. Let us write

v_{j}=\sum_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{j}}c_{b}G^{\imath}_{0}(b)

with
c_{b}\in\mathbf{K}
. Set
d:=\max\{\deg(c_{b})\mid b\in\bigsqcup_{j}\mathcal{B}_{j}\}
. Assume that
d>0
. Then, we have

v_{j}\in\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathcal{B}_{j}\\
\deg(c_{b})=d\end{subarray}}\operatorname{lt}(c_{b})G^{\imath}_{0}(b)+q^{d-1}\mathcal{L}_{M}.

This shows that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\bigsqcup_{j}\mathcal{B}_{j}\\
\deg(c_{b})=d\end{subarray}}\operatorname{lc}(c_{b})G^{\imath}_{0}(b)\equiv q^{-d}v\equiv 0

modulo
q^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{M}
. Taking
\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}
, we obtain

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\bigsqcup_{j}\mathcal{B}_{j}\\
\deg(c_{b})=d\end{subarray}}\operatorname{lc}(c_{b})b=0,

and hence,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\bigsqcup_{j}\mathcal{B}_{j}\\
\deg(c_{b})=d\end{subarray}}\operatorname{lt}(c_{b})G^{\imath}_{0}(b)=q^{d}G^{\imath}_{0}(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\bigsqcup_{j}\mathcal{B}_{j}\\
\deg(c_{b})=d\end{subarray}}\operatorname{lc}(c_{b})b)=0.

Replacing
v_{j}
with

v_{j}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathcal{B}_{j}\\
\deg(c_{b})=d\end{subarray}}\operatorname{lt}(c_{b})G^{\imath}_{0}(b)\in\widetilde{Y}_{j}V(\nu)_{\xi+\gamma_{j}},

we still have
v=\sum_{j}v_{j}
. Furthermore, if we write
v_{j}=\sum_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{j}}c_{b}G^{\imath}_{0}(b)
again, then, we have

\max\{\deg(c_{b})\mid b\in\bigsqcup_{j}\mathcal{B}_{j}\}<d.

Repeating this procedure, we conclude that
v_{j}\in\mathcal{L}(\nu)
for all
j
, as desired.

Now, we have
v=\sum_{j}v_{j}
with
v_{j}\in\widetilde{Y}_{j}\mathcal{L}(\nu)_{\xi+\gamma_{j}}
. Taking
\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}
, we see that
b\in\sum_{j}\widetilde{Y}_{j}\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\xi+\gamma_{j}}
. This shows that

\mathcal{L}(\nu)_{\xi}=\sum_{j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}}\widetilde{Y}_{j}\mathcal{L}(\nu)_{\xi+\gamma_{j}},\quad\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\xi}=\sum_{j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}}\widetilde{Y}_{j}\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\xi+\gamma_{j}}.

Then, by Lemma
8.1.1
, we obtain

G^{\imath}(\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\xi})\subseteq V(\nu)

Now, the assertions, except (
3
), follow.

Let us prove (
3
). Let
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)
be such that
\widetilde{X}_{j}b=0
for all
j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}
. Then, by Propositions
6.1.5
and
7.1.5
, for each
b^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)
we have

(b,\widetilde{Y}_{j}b^{\prime})_{\nu}=(\widetilde{X}_{j}b,b^{\prime})_{\nu}=0.

This shows that

(b,\bigoplus_{\xi<\nu}\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\xi})_{\nu}=0.

By weight consideration, we obtain
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\nu}=\mathbb{C}b_{\nu}
, as desired.
∎

Corollary 8.1.3
.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional based classical weight standard
X^{\imath}
-weight module satisfying condition (
46
). Then, for each
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
, the following hold.

(1)

M[\geq\nu]
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-submodule of
M
.

(2)

M[>\nu]
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-submodule of
M
and
M[\geq\nu]
.

(3)

M[\geq\nu]/M[>\nu]
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module isomorphic to
V(\nu)^{m_{\nu}}
, where
m_{\nu}
denotes the multiplicity
\dim_{\mathbf{K}}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{U}^{\imath}}(V(\nu),M)
of
V(\nu)
in
M
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Lemma
6.4.4
and Theorem
8.1.2
.
∎

8.2.
Branching rule

In this subsection, we prove a combinatorial formula describing the branching rule from
\mathbf{U}
to
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
, which coincides with that from
\mathfrak{g}
to
\mathfrak{k}
. Before doing so, let us explain that our formula also describes the branching rule from
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
to
\mathfrak{so}_{n}
. Recall that
\mathfrak{g}(I_{\mathfrak{k}})
denote the complex simple Lie algebra of type
D_{m}
if
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}
or
B_{m}
if
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}
. Let
e^{\prime}_{i},f^{\prime}_{i},h^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathfrak{g}(I_{\mathfrak{k}})
,
i\in I_{\mathfrak{k}}
denote the Chevalley generators. Then, we have seen that there exist isomorphisms
\mathfrak{g}(I_{\mathfrak{k}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{k}
and
\mathfrak{g}(I_{\mathfrak{k}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{so}_{n}
which send
h^{\prime}_{i}
to
w_{i}
and
w^{\prime}_{i}
, respectively (see Subsection
4.1
for the definitions of
w_{i}
and
w^{\prime}_{i}
). Namely, we have two realizations of
\mathfrak{g}(I_{\mathfrak{k}})
inside
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
. By
[
24
, 2.4]
and the following lemma, we see that the branching rules from
\mathfrak{g}
to
\mathfrak{k}
and from
\mathfrak{g}
to
\mathfrak{so}_{n}
coincide.

Lemma 8.2.1
.

Let
V:=\mathbb{C}^{n}
denote the natural representation of
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
. Then, as
\mathfrak{g}(I_{\mathfrak{k}})
-modules, we have
V|_{\mathfrak{k}}\simeq V|_{\mathfrak{so}_{n}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
V|_{\mathfrak{k}}
and
V|_{\mathfrak{so}_{n}}
have the same character. Recall that the subspaces
\mathbb{C}\{b_{2i-1}\mid i\in[1,m]\}
and
\mathbb{C}\{b^{\prime}_{2i-1}\mid i\in[1,m]\}
form Cartan subalgebras of
\mathfrak{k}
and
\mathfrak{so}_{n}
, respectively. Under the isomorphisms
\mathfrak{g}(I_{\mathfrak{k}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{k}
and
\mathfrak{g}(I_{\mathfrak{k}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{so}_{n}
, for each
i\in[1,m]
,
b_{2i-1}
and
\sqrt{-1}b^{\prime}_{2i-1}
correspond to the same vector in
\mathfrak{g}(I_{\mathfrak{k}})
. Hence, in order to prove the assertion, it suffices to show that for each
i\in[1,m]
,
b_{2i-1}
and
\sqrt{-1}b^{\prime}_{2i-1}
have the same spectra on
V
.

Let
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\}
denote the standard basis of
V=\mathbb{C}^{n}
. Then, for each
i\in[1,m]
such that
2i\leq n
, the vector
v_{2i-1}\pm v_{2i}
(resp.,
v_{2i-1}\pm\sqrt{-1}v_{2i}
) is an engenvector of
b_{2j-1}
(resp.,
\sqrt{-1}b^{\prime}_{2j-1}
) of eigenvalue
\pm\delta_{i,j}
. Also, if
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}
, the vector
v_{n}
is an eigenvector of both
b_{2j-1}
and
\sqrt{-1}b^{\prime}_{2j-1}
of eigenvalue
0
. Therefore, our claim follows. Thus, the proof completes.
∎

Remark 8.2.2
.

Another often used realization of
\mathfrak{g}(I_{\mathfrak{k}})
is

\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}:=\{X=(x_{i,j})_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\in\mathfrak{g}\mid x_{i,j}=-x_{n-j+1,n-i+1}\},

with
h^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathfrak{g}(I_{\mathfrak{k}})
corresponding to
h_{i}+h_{n-i}
if
i\neq m
,
h_{m-1}+2h_{m}+h_{m+1}
if
i=m
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}
, or
2(h_{m}+h_{m+1})
if
i=m
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}
. By the same way as above, we see that the branching rule from
\mathfrak{g}
to
\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}
is the same as that from
\mathfrak{g}
to
\mathfrak{k}
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{+}
and consider the irreducible
\mathfrak{g}
-module
V_{\mathfrak{g}}(\lambda)
of highest weight
\lambda
. As a
\mathfrak{k}
-module, it decomposes as

V_{\mathfrak{g}}(\lambda)\simeq\bigoplus_{\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}}V_{\mathfrak{k}}(\nu)^{\oplus[\lambda:\nu]}

for some
[\lambda:\nu]\geq 0
.

Also, consider the irreducible
\mathbf{U}
-module
V(\lambda)
, and set
V(\lambda)_{\mathbf{K}_{1}}:=\mathbf{U}_{\mathbf{K}_{1}}v_{\lambda}
. We have

V(\lambda)_{1}:=V(\lambda)_{\mathbf{K}_{1}}/(q-1)V(\lambda)_{\mathbf{K}_{1}}\simeq V_{\mathfrak{g}}(\lambda).

Let
v\in V(\lambda)_{\mathbf{K}_{1}}
and write its
X_{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight vector decomposition as
v=\sum_{\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}}v_{\xi}
with
v_{\xi}\in V(\lambda)_{\xi}
. Then, for each
\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}
, we have for sufficiently large
N\geq 0
,

\prod_{i\in I_{\otimes}}\prod_{a\in[-N,N]\setminus\{\xi_{i}\}}(B_{i}-[a])v=\prod_{i\in I_{\otimes}}\prod_{a\in[-N,N]\setminus\{\xi_{i}\}}([\xi_{i}]-[a])v_{\xi}.

Hence, we obtain

v_{\xi}=\prod_{i\in I_{\otimes}}\prod_{a\in[-N,N]\setminus\{\xi_{i}\}}\frac{1}{[\xi_{i}]-[a]}(B_{i}-[a])v\in\mathbf{U}_{\mathbf{K}_{1}}v_{\lambda}\subseteq V(\lambda)_{\mathbf{K}_{1}}.

This shows that

V(\lambda)_{\mathbf{K}_{1}}=\bigoplus_{\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}}(V(\lambda)_{\xi}\cap V(\lambda)_{\mathbf{K}_{1}}),

and hence,

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathbf{U}^{\imath}}V(\lambda)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathfrak{k}}V_{\mathfrak{g}}(\lambda).

By character consideration, we have

V(\lambda)\simeq\bigoplus_{\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}}V(\nu)^{\oplus[\lambda:\nu]},

and hence,

\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\lambda)\simeq\bigoplus_{\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)^{\oplus[\lambda:\nu]}.

This, together with Theorem
8.1.2
(
3
), shows that

[\lambda:\nu]=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\{b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\lambda)_{\nu}\mid\widetilde{X}_{j}b=0\text{ for all }j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}\}.

However, we do not know how
\widetilde{X}_{j}
acts on
\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\lambda)
explicitly. In the sequel, we aim to describe this multiplicity
[\lambda;\nu]
in terms of
\deg_{i}
and
\widetilde{B}_{i}
, which in turn, can be described in terms of the crystal structure of
\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
by Corollary
5.1.6
.

Lemma 8.2.3
.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional
\mathbf{U}
-module with a crystal basis
\mathcal{B}
. Let
b\in\mathcal{B}
, and write its
X_{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight vector decomposition as
b=\sum_{\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}}b_{\nu}
with
b_{\nu}\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M,\nu}
. Then, we have
b_{\nu}\neq 0
if and only if
|\nu_{2i-1}|=\deg_{2i-1}(b)
for all
i\in[1,m]
. Furthermore, if
b_{\nu}\neq 0
, then we have

b_{\nu}=\frac{1}{2^{s}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[1,m]\\
\nu_{2i-1}>0\end{subarray}}(1+\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[1,m]\\
\nu_{2i-1}<0\end{subarray}}(1-\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})b,

where
s:=\sharp\{i\mid\nu_{2i-1}\neq 0\}
.

Proof.

Let
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}
be such that
|\nu_{2i-1}|=\deg_{2i-1}(b)
for all
i\in[1,m]
, and set
s
to be the number of
i
such that
\nu_{2i-1}\neq 0
. By Lemma
5.1.3
and Corollary
5.1.6
, we see that

\frac{1}{2^{s}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[1,m]\\
\nu_{2i-1}>0\end{subarray}}(1+\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[1,m]\\
\nu_{2i-1}<0\end{subarray}}(1-\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})b

is a nonzero
X_{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight vector of weight
\nu
. Also, by an elementary calculation, we have

b=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}\\
|\nu_{2i-1}|=\deg_{2i-1}(b)\text{ for all }i\in[1,m]\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{s}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[1,m]\\
\nu_{2i-1}>0\end{subarray}}(1+\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[1,m]\\
\nu_{2i-1}<0\end{subarray}}(1-\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})b.

Therefore, this is the
X_{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight decomposition of
b
. Thus, the proof completes.
∎

For a finite-dimensional classical weight module
M
equipped with a contragredient Hermitian inner product, and
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}
, consider the following condition:

(51)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{$b$ is homogeneous, i.e., $B_{i}$-homogeneous for all $i\in[1,n-1]$},\\
&\deg_{2i}(b)=0\quad\text{ for all }i\in[1,m]\text{ such that }2i<n,\\
&\deg_{2i+1}((\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{\deg_{2i+1}(b)}b)=0\quad\text{ for all }i\in[1,m]\text{ such that }2i+1<n.\end{split}

Proposition 8.2.4
.

Let
M
be a finite-dimensional classical weight module equipped with a contragredient Hermitian inner product. Set

\displaystyle\begin{split}&L_{1}:=\{b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}\mid\widetilde{X}_{j}b=0\text{ for all }j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}\},\\
&L_{2}:=\mathbb{C}\{b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{M}\mid\text{ $b$ satisfies condition \eqref{combinatorial characterization}}\}.\end{split}

Then, the linear map

L_{2}\rightarrow L_{1};\ b\mapsto(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})(1+\widetilde{B}_{3})\cdots(1+\widetilde{B}_{2m^{\prime}-1})b,

where

m^{\prime}:=\begin{cases}m-1\quad&\text{ if }n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}},\\
m\quad&\text{ if }n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}\end{cases}

is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}
-vector spaces with inverse

L_{1}\rightarrow L_{2};\ b\mapsto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{2i}=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m^{\prime}]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}b.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
m^{\prime}
. The
m^{\prime}=1
case follows from Propositions
6.3.8
and
7.2.6
.

As the proof of Proposition
6.3.8
, it suffices to consider the case when
M=V(\nu)
for some
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
. Clearly, we have

L_{1}=\mathbb{C}b_{\nu}.

As the proof of Lemma
6.4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}c^{\nu}&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{2i}=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m^{\prime}]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}b_{\nu}\\
&=\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{2i}=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m^{\prime}]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}v_{\nu})\\
&=\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(\mathbf{1}_{1,\overline{\nu_{1}}}\mathbf{1}_{2,\overline{0}}\mathbf{1}_{3,\overline{\nu_{3}}}\mathbf{1}_{4,\overline{0}}\cdots\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime}-1,\overline{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}}}\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime},\overline{0}}v_{\nu})\\
&=\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(\frac{1}{2^{m^{\prime}}}(1+(-1)^{\nu_{1}}Y_{2}^{(2\nu_{1})})(1+(-1)^{\nu_{3}}Y_{4}^{(2\nu_{3})})\cdots(1+(-1)^{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}}Y_{2m^{\prime}}^{(2\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1})})v_{\nu})\\
&=\frac{1}{2^{m^{\prime}}}(1+(-1)^{\nu_{1}}\widetilde{Y}_{2}^{2\nu_{1}})(1+(-1)^{\nu_{3}}\widetilde{Y}_{4}^{2\nu_{3}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}}\widetilde{Y}_{2m^{\prime}}^{2\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}})b_{\nu}\neq 0.\end{split}

We show that
c^{\nu}\in L_{2}
. As a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{1,2,\ldots,2m^{\prime}-1}
-module vector, both
v_{\nu}
and
Y_{2m^{\prime}}^{(2\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1})}v_{\nu}
are highest weight vectors of weights
(\nu_{1},\nu_{3},\ldots,\nu_{2m^{\prime}-3},\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1})
and
(\nu_{1},\nu_{3},\ldots,\nu_{2m^{\prime}-3},-\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1})
, respectively. By the induction hypothesis, we see that
c^{\nu}
satisfies condition (
51
) as a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{1,2,\ldots,2m^{\prime}-1}
-module vector.

On the other hand, by the definition of
\mathbf{1}_{i,p}
’s (
i\in I
,
p\in\{\mathrm{ev},\mathrm{odd}\}
), they pairwise commute. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{1}_{1,\overline{\nu_{1}}}\mathbf{1}_{2,\overline{0}}\mathbf{1}_{3,\overline{\nu_{3}}}\mathbf{1}_{4,\overline{0}}\cdots\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime}-1,\overline{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}}}\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime},\overline{0}}v_{\nu}\\
&=\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime}-1,\overline{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}}}\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime},\overline{0}}\mathbf{1}_{1,\overline{\nu_{1}}}\mathbf{1}_{2,\overline{0}}\mathbf{1}_{3,\overline{\nu_{3}}}\mathbf{1}_{4,\overline{0}}\cdots\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime}-3,\overline{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-3}}}\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime}-2,\overline{0}}v_{\nu}\\
&=\frac{1}{2^{m^{\prime}-1}}\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime}-1,\overline{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}}}\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime},\overline{0}}\\
&\quad\cdot(1+(-1)^{\nu_{1}}Y_{2}^{(2\nu_{1})})(1+(-1)^{\nu_{3}}Y_{4}^{(2\nu_{3})})\cdots(1+(-1)^{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-3}}Y_{2m^{\prime}-2}^{(2\nu_{2m^{\prime}-3})})v_{\nu},\end{split}

and consequently,

\displaystyle\begin{split}c^{\nu}&=\frac{1}{2^{m^{\prime}-1}}\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime}-1,\overline{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}}}\mathbf{1}_{2m^{\prime},\overline{0}}\\
&\quad\cdot(1+(-1)^{\nu_{1}}\widetilde{Y}_{2}^{2\nu_{1}})(1+(-1)^{\nu_{3}}\widetilde{Y}_{4}^{2\nu_{3}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-3}}\widetilde{Y}_{2m^{\prime}-2}^{2\nu_{2m^{\prime}-3}})b_{\nu}.\end{split}

Now, set

J:=\begin{cases}\{2m^{\prime}-1,2m^{\prime},2m^{\prime}+1\}\quad&\text{ if }n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}},\\
\{2m^{\prime}-1,2m^{\prime}\}\quad&\text{ if }n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}.\end{cases}

By weight consideration, as a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{J}
-module vector,

(1+(-1)^{\nu_{1}}Y_{2}^{(2\nu_{1})})(1+(-1)^{\nu_{3}}Y_{4}^{(2\nu_{3})})\cdots(1+(-1)^{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-3}}Y_{2m^{\prime}-2}^{(2\nu_{2m^{\prime}-3})})v_{\nu}

is a sum of highest weight vectors of weight
(\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1},\nu_{2m^{\prime}+1})
and a lowest weight vector of weight
(-\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1},\nu_{2m^{\prime}+1})
if
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}
, or a sum of highest weight vectors of weight
\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}
and lowest weight vectors of weight
-\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}
if
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}
. Here, lowest weight vector means a vector of the form
Y^{(2\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1})}v
for some highest weight vector
v
. Then, by Proposition
6.3.7
, we see that
c^{\nu}
is a linear combination of vectors of the form

(1+(-1)^{\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}}\widetilde{Y})b^{\prime}

with
b^{\prime}
being a highest weight vector of weight
(\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1},\pm\nu_{2m^{\prime}+1})
if
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}
, or
\nu_{2m^{\prime}-1}
if
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}
. Therefore, by the
n=3,4
cases,
c^{\nu}
satisfies condition (
51
) as a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{J}
-module vector. Thus, we conclude that

c^{\nu}\in L_{2},

as desired.

Next, let
c\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)
satisfy condition (
51
). As the proof of Lemma
8.2.3
, we can write the
X_{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight vector decomposition of
c
as

c=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}\\
|\xi_{2k-1}|=\deg_{2k-1}(c)\text{ for all }k\in[1,m]\end{subarray}}c_{\xi},\quad c_{\xi}\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\nu)_{\xi}.

Let
i\in[1,m^{\prime}]
. Then, we have

(1+\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})c=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\xi_{2k-1}|=\deg_{2k-1}(c)\text{ for all }k\in[1,m]\\
\xi_{2i-1}\geq 0\end{subarray}}2^{1-\delta_{\deg_{2i-1}(c),0}}c_{\xi}.

Define
J_{i}\subseteq I
by

J_{i}:=\begin{cases}\{2i-1,2i,2i+1\}\quad&\text{ if }2i+1<n,\\
\{2i-1,2i\}\quad&\text{ if }2i+1=n.\end{cases}

Then, by the
n=3,4
cases, we have

\widetilde{X}_{j}(1+\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})c=0\quad\text{ for all }j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}(J_{i}).

By weight consideration, we see that

(52)

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}_{j}c_{\xi}=0\quad\text{ for all }j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}(J_{i})\and\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}\text{ such that }\xi_{2i-1}=\deg_{2i-1}(c).

Now, remark that

c^{\prime}:=(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})(1+\widetilde{B}_{3})\cdots(1+\widetilde{B}_{2m^{\prime}-1})c=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\xi_{2k-1}|=\deg_{2k-1}(c)\text{ for all }k\in[1,m]\\
\xi_{2i-1}\geq 0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m^{\prime}]\end{subarray}}2^{s}c_{\xi},

where
s=\sharp\{i\in[1,m^{\prime}]\mid\deg_{2i-1}(c)\neq 0\}
. By (
52
), we conclude that

\widetilde{X}_{j}c^{\prime}=0\text{ for all }j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}},

and hence,

c^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}b_{\nu}=L_{1}

by Theorem
8.1.2
(
3
).

This far, we have verified that the linear maps in consideration are well-defined, and that
\dim L_{2}\geq\dim L_{1}=1
. Hence, in order to complete the proof, it suffices to show that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{2i}=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m^{\prime}]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})(1+\widetilde{B}_{3})\cdots(1+\widetilde{B}_{2m^{\prime}-1})c=c.

Set

J^{\prime}:=\{1,2,3\},\quad J^{\prime\prime}:=\{3,4,\ldots,n-1\}.

It is easily verified that
(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})c
satisfies condition (
51
) as a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{J^{\prime\prime}}
-module vector. Then, by the induction hypothesis, we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta^{\prime\prime}\in X^{\imath}(J^{\prime\prime})\\
\zeta_{2i}=0\text{ for all }i\in[2,m^{\prime}]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{J^{\prime\prime},\zeta^{\prime\prime}}(1+\widetilde{B}_{3})(1+\widetilde{B}_{5})\cdots(1+\widetilde{B}_{2m^{\prime}-1})(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})c=(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})c.

Hence, we compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{2i}=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m^{\prime}]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})(1+\widetilde{B}_{3})\cdots(1+\widetilde{B}_{2m^{\prime}-1})c\\
&\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta^{\prime}\in X^{\imath}(J^{\prime})\\
\zeta_{2}=0\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta^{\prime\prime}\in X^{\imath}(J^{\prime\prime})\\
\zeta_{2i}=0\text{ for all }i\in[2,m^{\prime}]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{J^{\prime},\zeta^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{J^{\prime\prime},\zeta^{\prime\prime}}(1+\widetilde{B}_{3})(1+\widetilde{B}_{5})\cdots(1+\widetilde{B}_{2m^{\prime}-1})(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})c\\
&\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta^{\prime}\in X^{\imath}(J^{\prime})\\
\zeta_{2}=0\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{J^{\prime},\zeta^{\prime}}(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})c.\end{split}

By the
n=4
case, the last term equals
c
, as desired. Thus, the proof completes.
∎

Lemma 8.2.5
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{+}
. Let
b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\lambda)
and write
b=\sum_{k=1}^{r}a_{k}b_{k}
with
a_{k}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
,
b_{k}\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
. If
b
satisfies condition (
51
), then so does
b_{k}
for all
k\in[1,r]
.

Proof.

Since each element in
\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
is homogeneous, we must have
\deg_{i}(b_{k})=\deg_{i}(b)
for all
i\in[1,n-1]
. In particular, we obtain

\deg_{2i}(b_{k})=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m^{\prime}].

Next, let
i\in I_{\mathfrak{k}}
be such that
2i+1<n
, and set
\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{l}\}\subseteq[1,r]
to be the subset such that
(\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{\deg_{2i+1}(b)}b_{k_{j}}\neq 0
and
\deg_{2i+1}((\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{\deg_{2i+1}(b)}b_{k_{j}})\neq 0
. Since
\deg_{2i+1}((\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{\deg_{2i+1}(b)}b)=0
, we must have

\sum_{j=1}^{l}a_{k_{j}}(\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{\deg_{2i+1}(b)}b_{k_{j}}=0.

Since
(\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{\deg_{2i+1}(b)}b_{k_{j}}
are distinct vectors in
\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
, we see that
(\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{\deg_{2i+1}(b)}b_{k_{j}}=0
for all
j\in[1,l]
. Therefore, we conclude that
\deg_{2i+1}((\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{\deg_{2i+1}(b)}b_{k})=0
for all
k\in[1,r]
. Thus, the proof completes.
∎

Theorem 8.2.6
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{+}
and
\nu\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
.

(1)

Suppose
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}
and
\nu_{2m-1}\neq 0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}[\lambda:\nu]=\frac{1}{2}\sharp\{b\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)\mid&\deg_{2i-1}(b)=|\nu_{2i-1}|\text{ for all }i\in[1,m],\\
&\deg_{2i}(b)=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m-1],\\
&\deg_{2i+1}((\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{|\nu_{2i+1}|}b)=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m-1]\}.\end{split}

(2)

Suppose
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}
and
\nu_{2m-1}=0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}[\lambda:\nu]=\sharp\{b\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)\mid&\deg_{2i-1}(b)=|\nu_{2i-1}|\text{ for all }i\in[1,m],\\
&\deg_{2i}(b)=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m-1],\\
&\deg_{2i+1}((\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{\nu_{2i+1}}b)=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m-1]\}.\end{split}

(3)

Suppose
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{odd}}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}[\lambda:\nu]=\sharp\{b\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)\mid&\deg_{2i-1}(b)=|\nu_{2i-1}|\text{ for all }i\in[1,m],\\
&\deg_{2i}(b)=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m],\\
&\deg_{2i+1}((\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{\nu_{2i+1}}b)=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m-1]\}.\end{split}

Proof.

Let
b\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
be such that

|\deg_{1}(b)|\geq|\deg_{3}(b)|\geq\cdots\geq|\deg_{2m-1}(b)|.

By Lemma
8.2.3
, we can write

b=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}\\
|\xi_{2i-1}|=\deg_{2i-1}(b)\text{ for all }i\in[1,m]\end{subarray}}b_{\xi},\quad b_{\xi}\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\lambda)_{\xi}\setminus\{0\}.

Note that when
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}
and
\deg_{2m-1}(b)\neq 0
, there are exactly two dominant weights
\xi^{+},\xi^{-}\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
such that
\xi^{+}_{2m-1}>0
,
\xi^{-}_{2m-1}<0
, and
b_{\xi^{+}},b_{\xi^{-}}\neq 0
. Otherwise, there is exactly one dominant weight
\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}^{+}
such that
b_{\xi}\neq 0
.

From now on, we concentrate on the case when
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}
and
\nu_{2m-1}\neq 0
. The other cases can be proved in a similar way more easily.

Set

L^{\prime}:=\{b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\lambda)_{\nu^{+}}\oplus\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\lambda)_{\nu^{-}}\mid\widetilde{X}_{j}b=0\text{ for all }j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}\},

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}B:=\{b\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)\mid&\deg_{2i-1}(b)=|\nu_{2i-1}|\text{ for all }i\in[1,m],\\
&\deg_{2i}(b)=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m-1],\\
&\deg_{2i+1}((\widetilde{B}_{2i}\widetilde{B}_{2i-1})^{|\nu_{2i+1}|}b)=0\text{ for all }i\in[1,m-1]\}.\end{split}

By Theorem
8.2.4
, we have an isomorphism

\mathbb{C}B\rightarrow L^{\prime}

which sends

b\mapsto(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})(1+\widetilde{B}_{3})\cdots(1+\widetilde{B}_{2m-3})b,

with inverse given by

b\mapsto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{2i}=\overline{0}\text{ for all }i\in[1,m-1]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}b.

Let
b\in B
and write its
X_{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight vector decomposition as

b=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}\\
|\xi_{2i-1}|=\deg_{2i-1}(b)\text{ for all }i\in[1,m]\end{subarray}}b_{\xi},\quad b_{\xi}\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\lambda)_{\xi}\setminus\{0\}.

Set

b^{+}:=\sum_{\xi_{1}\cdot\xi_{3}\cdots\xi_{2m-1}>0}b_{\xi},\quad b^{-}:=\sum_{\xi_{1}\cdot\xi_{3}\cdots\xi_{2m-1}<0}b_{\xi}.

Let us show that
b^{\pm}\in\mathbb{C}B
.

By the definitions, we have

b=b^{+}+b^{-},

and

(1+\widetilde{B}_{1})(1+\widetilde{B}_{3})\cdots(1+\widetilde{B}_{2m-3})b^{\pm}=2^{m-1}b_{\nu^{\pm}}.

Since we have
\widetilde{X}_{j}(b_{\nu^{+}}+b_{\nu^{-}})=0
for all
j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, by weight consideration, we obtain

\widetilde{X}_{j}b_{\nu^{\pm}}=0\text{ for all }j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}.

In particular, we have

b_{\nu^{\pm}}\in L^{\prime}.

On the other hand, we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{2i}=\overline{0}\text{ for all }i\in[1,m-1]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}2^{m-1}(b_{\nu^{+}}+b_{\nu^{-}})=b=b^{+}+b^{-}.

Also, since
b_{\nu^{\pm}}
is a highest weight vector of weight
\nu^{\pm}
, we see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{2i}=\overline{0}\text{ for all }i\in[1,m-1]\end{subarray}}&\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}2^{m-1}b_{\nu^{\pm}}\\
&=(1+(-1)^{\nu_{1}}\widetilde{Y}_{2}^{2\nu_{1}})(1+(-1)^{\nu_{3}}\widetilde{Y}_{4}^{2\nu_{3}})\cdots(1+(-1)^{\nu_{2m-3}}\widetilde{Y}_{2m-2}^{2\nu_{2m-3}})b_{\nu^{\pm}}\end{split}

is a sum of
X_{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight vectors of weights
\xi^{\prime}\in X_{\mathfrak{k},\operatorname{int}}
with
\pm\xi^{\prime}_{1}\cdot\xi^{\prime}_{3}\cdots\xi^{\prime}_{2m-1}>0
. Therefore, we see that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in X^{\imath}\\
\zeta_{2i}=\overline{0}\text{ for all }i\in[1,m-1]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}2^{m-1}b_{\nu^{\pm}}=b^{\pm}.

This shows that

b^{\pm}\in\mathbb{C}B,

as desired.

Now, we show that
\widetilde{B}_{1}\widetilde{B}_{3}\cdots\widetilde{B}_{2m-1}
restricts to an involution on
B
. In fact, for each
b=b^{+}+b^{-}\in B
, we have

\widetilde{B}_{1}\widetilde{B}_{3}\cdots\widetilde{B}_{2m-1}b=b^{+}-b^{-}\in\mathbb{C}B.

Since
\widetilde{B}_{1}\widetilde{B}_{3}\cdots\widetilde{B}_{2m-1}b\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
, we conclude that
\widetilde{B}_{1}\widetilde{B}_{3}\cdots\widetilde{B}_{2m-1}b\in B
.

Also, we have
\widetilde{B}_{1}\widetilde{B}_{3}\cdots\widetilde{B}_{2m-1}b=b
if and only if
b^{-}=0
, which cannot occur by Lemma
8.2.3
. Thus, we obtain an isomorphism

\mathbb{C}\{(1\pm\widetilde{B}_{1}\widetilde{B}_{3}\cdots\widetilde{B}_{2m-1})b\mid b\in B\}\rightarrow L^{\prime}_{\pm}:=\{b\in\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\lambda)_{\nu^{\pm}}\mid\widetilde{X}_{j}b=0\text{ for all }j\in\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{k}}\}.

This implies

[\lambda:\nu^{\pm}]=\dim L^{\prime}_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}|B|.

Since we have either
\nu=\nu^{+}
or
\nu=\nu^{-}
, the assertion follows.
∎
